I mean, I guess I'm the one physically doing it, but... it's like I was saying, it just feels like my brain goes on autopilot. I know I shouldn't be eating that much, and I can literally see my hand grabbing another handful of chips or whatever, but I just can't seem to hit the brakes. \n\nIt's probably just a lack of willpower on my part, right? I'm sure you hear that all the time. I just... I want to be able to stop, but in those moments, it's like I'm watching someone else do it. Does that sound crazy? I really hope I'm not wasting your time with this.\n\nDoctor: Are there specific types of food that you tend to eat when this happens?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I don't want you to think badly of me, but sometimes, after everyone else has gone to bed, I... I'll go into the bathroom and... well, I might make myself throw up. Or, you know, sometimes I'll take a few laxatives. Um... no, not always. I mean, like I said, I don't always throw up or take the laxatives. Sometimes I just... I try to handle it in a healthier way? I guess.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, no, not every single time. Like I said, sometimes I just walk or skip meals. But... I mean, yes, sometimes I do. 

Usually it's late, after everyone else has gone to bed. If I've had one of those bad episodes, I'll go into the bathroom and... I'll make myself throw up, or I'll take some laxatives. I just tell myself it's only to undo the damage, you know? Just to sort of balance things out. 

But I always feel really embarrassed afterward... and a bit shaky, honestly. It's not something I'm proud of. I know it's not the best way to handle it, but... sometimes it just feels like the only option in the moment. That's not too terrible, is it? Um... well, it's a bit of both, I suppose, but... mostly it's just about the weight. I mean, I know I just talked about this, but... yeah, when it's late and everyone is asleep, and I've had one of those bad nights... I go into the bathroom and make myself throw up, or take the laxatives.

Is that... is that the kind of thing you were asking?\n\nDoctor: How do you feel after one of these eating episodes, once you've stopped eating?\n\nPatient: Um... well, after I stop eating, I usually feel pretty terrible. Physically and, you know, emotionally. I just feel so full and uncomfortable, and I get really down on myself for letting it happen again. \n\nAnd... um... well, sometimes... I don't want you to think badly of me, but sometimes, after everyone else has gone to bed, I... I'll go into the bathroom and... well, I might make myself throw up. Or, you know, sometimes I'll take a few laxatives. It's not every single time, and I know it's probably not the best thing to do, but in the moment, I just tell myself I need to undo the damage, you know? Just kind of reset things. \n\nAfterward, I always feel so shaky and just... really embarrassed about the whole thing. I'm sorry, I know that's probably not what you want to hear from a patient. Does that make sense, though? I hope I'm not making things too complicated.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating and then purging happen?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's really not *that* often, I promise. Just... I guess for the last few months, it's been happening maybe once a week? Most weeks, anyway. I'll have one of those nights where the eating just kind of gets away from me, and then... well, then I'll try to fix it. \n\nI might throw up, like I said, or maybe take a laxative. But sometimes I just try to be really good the next day, you know? Like, I'll just skip meals entirely, or I'll force myself to go on a really, really long walk to burn it off. Just to make up for it, so it's not a big deal. \n\nI really try to keep it under control, I swear. Is... is once a week considered really bad? I hope I'm not, like, your worst case or anything. I'm really trying to do better, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, my weight stays pretty much the same, mostly. I think I might fluctuate just a little bit, maybe a pound or two here and there, but nothing major at all. I try really hard to keep it steady, you know? \n\nI... I hope that's okay? Like, it's a good thing that my weight isn't changing too much, right? I'm trying not to let things get too out of hand. I really want to be doing the right thing here.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating and then purging usually happen when you're feeling stressed, anxious, or upset?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess sometimes? But I wouldn't say it's always because I'm upset or anything like that. I mean, everybody gets a little stressed out sometimes, right? Work can be a bit much, just dealing with customers all day, but I really try to leave it at the door when I come home. \n\nSometimes it just... happens, even if I'm having a perfectly okay day. I don't want you to think I'm, like, a total mess who can't handle his emotions or anything. I'm usually pretty level-headed. I just... I don't know, maybe I just get a little carried away sometimes? \n\nDoes that sound normal to you? I mean, I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is. I really do try to manage my stress well.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself throw up or take laxatives after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's really not every single time, I promise. Just... occasionally. Like, sometimes after everyone goes to bed, and the house is quiet, I might... I might go to the bathroom and make myself throw up. Or, um, maybe take some laxatives. \n\nI just tell myself it's only to undo the damage, you know? Just to sort of reset things so I don't gain weight. But... I always feel really embarrassed afterward. And a little shaky, honestly. It's not something I'm proud of at all. \n\nI really try not to do it, though. I know it's probably not the best way to handle things, right? I hope you don't think too badly of me for it... I'm really trying to be honest with you so I can get better. Is it... is it common for people to do that sometimes?\n\nDoctor: How many times a month do you make yourself throw up or take laxatives after eating?\n\nPatient: Um... if I really have to put a number on it... I guess, for the last few months, it's been happening most weeks. Just, like, maybe once a week I'll have one of those nights where the eating gets a little out of control. \n\nAnd then... yeah, I'll try to make up for it. Sometimes I'll make myself throw up, or take those laxatives like I mentioned. But other times I just try to be really good the next day, you know? Like, I'll skip some meals, or I'll force myself to go on a really long walk to try and burn it off. \n\nI just... I want to fix it so I don't ruin all my hard work. That's not too terrible, is it? I mean, at least I'm trying to balance it out, right? I hope that doesn't sound too awful... I really am trying my best here.\n\nDoctor: Do you always make yourself throw up or take laxatives after one of these episodes of eating?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not always. I mean, like I said, I don't always throw up or take the laxatives. Sometimes I just... I try to handle it in a healthier way? I guess. \n\nLike, if I have a night where I eat too much, the next day I'll just be really strict with myself. I'll usually just skip breakfast and lunch entirely. Or... or I'll make sure I go for a really, really long walk before my shift at the store. And I mean, I'm not doing it because I just love walking or anything... it's just, I feel like I absolutely have to, you know? Just to cancel out what I did the night before. \n\nThat's a bit better than throwing up, right? Like, at least fasting and walking are kind of normal things people do to watch their weight... right? I just want to make sure I'm not doing everything completely wrong.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself throw up or take laxatives when you haven't had one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, no. No, definitely not. If I haven't had one of those... those nights where I eat too much, I wouldn't do anything like that. \n\nI mean, I really only do it when I feel like I absolutely have to, just to fix a mistake. If I've been good and stuck to my normal eating, there's no reason to throw up or take anything. I'm really trying to be sensible about it most of the time. That makes sense, doesn't it? I wouldn't just do it for no reason... I'm not that far gone or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself throw up or take laxatives on days when you haven't eaten a lot?\n\nPatient: Um, no, definitely not. I mean, I think I just mentioned that, but... no. If I haven't eaten a lot, I wouldn't do anything like that. \n\nI only ever do it if I've really slipped up and eaten way too much. If I've had a normal day, or if I'm just eating my regular meals, I wouldn't take laxatives or anything. That wouldn't be right, would it? I just... I only do it to fix a mistake, not just out of nowhere. I hope that shows I still have some control over things, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you purge after every episode of eating where you feel like you've lost control?\n\nPatient: Um... well, no, not every single time. Just to sort of balance things out. \n\nBut I always feel really embarrassed afterward... and a bit shaky, honestly. It's not something I'm proud of. I mean, I know it's not great, but I'm trying to be honest with you.\n\nDoctor: When you make yourself throw up or take laxatives, is it mostly to prevent weight gain or to get rid of the feeling of being full?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's a bit of both, I suppose, but... mostly it's just about the weight. Um... well, yes, I suppose I do. But, like I was saying earlier, it's really not an everyday thing. I try very hard to keep a lid on it.

I really want to be doing the right thing here.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating and then purging usually happen when you're feeling stressed, anxious, or upset?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess sometimes? But I wouldn't say it's always because I'm upset or anything like that. I mean, everybody gets a little stressed out sometimes, right? Work can be a bit much, just dealing with customers all day, but I really try to leave it at the door when I come home. \n\nSometimes it just... happens, even if I'm having a perfectly okay day. I don't want you to think I'm, like, a total mess who can't handle his emotions or anything. I'm usually pretty level-headed. I just... I don't know, maybe I just get a little carried away sometimes? \n\nDoes that sound normal to you? I mean, I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is. I really do try to manage my stress well.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself throw up or take laxatives after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's really not every single time, I promise. Just... occasionally. Like, sometimes after everyone goes to bed, and the house is quiet, I might... I might go to the bathroom and make myself throw up. Or, um, maybe take some laxatives. \n\nI just tell myself it's only to undo the damage, you know? Just to sort of reset things so I don't gain weight. But... I always feel really embarrassed afterward. And a little shaky, honestly. It's not something I'm proud of at all. \n\nI really try not to do it, though. I know it's probably not the best way to handle things, right? I hope you don't think too badly of me for it... I'm really trying to be honest with you so I can get better. Work can be a bit much, just dealing with customers all day, but I really try to leave it at the door when I come home. \n\nSometimes it just... happens, even if I'm having a perfectly okay day. I don't want you to think I'm, like, a total mess who can't handle his emotions or anything. I'm usually pretty level-headed. I just... I don't know, maybe I just get a little carried away sometimes? \n\nDoes that sound normal to you? I mean, I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is. I really do try to manage my stress well.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself throw up or take laxatives after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's really not every single time, I promise. Just... occasionally. Like, sometimes after everyone goes to bed, and the house is quiet, I might... I might go to the bathroom and make myself throw up. Or, um, maybe take some laxatives. \n\nI just tell myself it's only to undo the damage, you know? Just to sort of reset things so I don't gain weight. But... I always feel really embarrassed afterward. And a little shaky, honestly. It's not something I'm proud of at all. \n\nI really try not to do it, though. I know it's probably not the best way to handle things, right? I hope you don't think too badly of me for it... I'm really trying to be honest with you so I can get better. Is it... is it common for people to do that sometimes?\n\nDoctor: How many times a month do you make yourself throw up or take laxatives after eating?\n\nPatient: Um... if I really have to put a number on it... I guess, for the last few months, it's been happening most weeks. Just, like, maybe once a week I'll have one of those nights where the eating gets a little out of control. \n\nAnd then... yeah, I'll try to make up for it. Sometimes I'll make myself throw up, or take those laxatives like I mentioned. But other times I just try to be really good the next day, you know? Like, I'll skip some meals, or I'll force myself to go on a really long walk to try and burn it off. \n\nI just... I want to fix it so I don't ruin all my hard work. That's not too terrible, is it? I mean, at least I'm trying to balance it out, right? I hope that doesn't sound too awful... I really am trying my best here.\n\nDoctor: Do you always make yourself throw up or take laxatives after one of these episodes of eating?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not always. I mean, like I said, I don't always throw up or take the laxatives. Sometimes I just... I try to handle it in a healthier way? I guess. \n\nLike, if I have a night where I eat too much, the next day I'll just be really strict with myself. I'll usually just skip breakfast and lunch entirely. Or... or I'll make sure I go for a really, really long walk before my shift at the store. And I mean, I'm not doing it because I just love walking or anything... it's just, I feel like I absolutely have to, you know? Just to cancel out what I did the night before. \n\nThat's a bit better than throwing up, right? Like, at least fasting and walking are kind of normal things people do to watch their weight... right? I just want to make sure I'm not doing everything completely wrong.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself throw up or take laxatives when you haven't had one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, no. No, definitely not. If I haven't had one of those... those nights where I eat too much, I wouldn't do anything like that. \n\nI mean, I really only do it when I feel like I absolutely have to, just to fix a mistake. If I've been good and stuck to my normal eating, there's no reason to throw up or take anything. I'm really trying to be sensible about it most of the time. That makes sense, doesn't it? I wouldn't just do it for no reason... I'm not that far gone or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself throw up or take laxatives on days when you haven't eaten a lot?\n\nPatient: Um, no, definitely not. I mean, I think I just mentioned that, but... no. If I haven't eaten a lot, I wouldn't do anything like that. \n\nI only ever do it if I've really slipped up and eaten way too much. If I've had a normal day, or if I'm just eating my regular meals, I wouldn't take laxatives or anything. That wouldn't be right, would it? I just... I only do it to fix a mistake, not just out of nowhere. I hope that shows I still have some control over things, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you purge after every episode of eating where you feel like you've lost control?\n\nPatient: Um... well, no, not every single time. Like I said, sometimes I just walk or skip meals. But... I mean, yes, sometimes I do. \n\nUsually it's late, after everyone else has gone to bed. If I've had one of those bad episodes, I'll go into the bathroom and... I'll make myself throw up, or I'll take some laxatives. I just tell myself it's only to undo the damage, you know? Just to sort of balance things out. \n\nBut I always feel really embarrassed afterward... and a bit shaky, honestly. It's not something I'm proud of. I know it's not the best way to handle it, but... sometimes it just feels like the only option in the moment. That's not too terrible, is it? I mean, I know it's not great, but I'm trying to be honest with you.\n\nDoctor: When you make yourself throw up or take laxatives, is it mostly to prevent weight gain or to get rid of the feeling of being full?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's a bit of both, I suppose, but... mostly it's just about the weight. I mean, I know I just talked about this, but... yeah, when it's late and everyone is asleep, and I've had one of those bad nights... That's not too terrible in the grand scheme of things, is it? I mean, it shows I'm not doing it all the time, right? I just tell myself it's only to undo the damage of what I consumed. I always feel so embarrassed about it afterward... and my body gets a little shaky. I really don't like doing it, I promise. I'm just trying to be as honest as I can with you. Am I explaining things okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: Do you purge after most of your binge eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... well, yes, I suppose I do. But, like I was saying earlier, it's really not an everyday thing. I mean, I guess I'm the one physically doing it, but... it's like I was saying, it just feels like my brain goes on autopilot. I know I shouldn't be eating that much, and I can literally see my hand grabbing another handful of chips or whatever, but I just can't seem to hit the brakes. \n\nIt's probably just a lack of willpower on my part, right? I'm sure you hear that all the time. I just... I want to be able to stop, but in those moments, it's like I'm watching someone else do it. Does that sound crazy? I really hope I'm not wasting your time with this.\n\nDoctor: Are there specific types of food that you tend to eat when this happens?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess it's usually just whatever is easy to grab. It's nothing too out of the ordinary, really. But... I suppose on a bad night, I might start with, like, a full plate of leftovers. And then... well, I'll just kind of keep going. I might go through some bread, maybe some snacks in the kitchen, and then some ice cream. \n\nAnd this all happens within, I don't know, an hour or two? It's... it's a bit much, I know. It's honestly way more than I'd ever eat if someone were actually sitting there in the room with me. I'm sorry, I know that probably sounds terrible. I hope I'm answering your question okay? Is that... is that the kind of thing you were asking?\n\nDoctor: How do you feel after one of these eating episodes, once you've stopped eating?\n\nPatient: Um... well, after I stop eating, I usually feel pretty terrible. Physically and, you know, emotionally. I just feel so full and uncomfortable, and I get really down on myself for letting it happen again. \n\nAnd... um... well, sometimes... I don't want you to think badly of me, but sometimes, after everyone else has gone to bed, I... I'll go into the bathroom and... well, I might make myself throw up. Or, you know, sometimes I'll take a few laxatives. It's not every single time, and I know it's probably not the best thing to do, but in the moment, I just tell myself I need to undo the damage, you know? Just kind of reset things. \n\nAfterward, I always feel so shaky and just... really embarrassed about the whole thing. I'm sorry, I know that's probably not what you want to hear from a patient. Does that make sense, though? I hope I'm not making things too complicated.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating and then purging happen?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's really not *that* often, I promise. Just... I guess for the last few months, it's been happening maybe once a week? Most weeks, anyway. I'll have one of those nights where the eating just kind of gets away from me, and then... well, then I'll try to fix it. \n\nI might throw up, like I said, or maybe take a laxative. But sometimes I just try to be really good the next day, you know? Like, I'll just skip meals entirely, or I'll force myself to go on a really, really long walk to burn it off. Just to make up for it, so it's not a big deal. \n\nI really try to keep it under control, I swear. Is... is once a week considered really bad? I hope I'm not, like, your worst case or anything. I'm really trying to do better, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, my weight stays pretty much the same, mostly. I think I might fluctuate just a little bit, maybe a pound or two here and there, but nothing major at all. I try really hard to keep it steady, you know? \n\nI... I hope that's okay? Like, it's a good thing that my weight isn't changing too much, right? I'm trying not to let things get too out of hand. I really want to be doing the right thing here.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating and then purging usually happen when you're feeling stressed, anxious, or upset?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess sometimes? But I wouldn't say it's always because I'm upset or anything like that. I mean, everybody gets a little stressed out sometimes, right? Work can be a bit much, just dealing with customers all day, but I really try to leave it at the door when I come home. \n\nSometimes it just... happens, even if I'm having a perfectly okay day. I don't want you to think I'm, like, a total mess who can't handle his emotions or anything. I'm usually pretty level-headed. I just... I don't know, maybe I just get a little carried away sometimes? \n\nDoes that sound normal to you? I mean, I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is. I really do try to manage my stress well.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself throw up or take laxatives after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's really not every single time, I promise. Just... occasionally. Like, sometimes after everyone goes to bed, and the house is quiet, I might... I might go to the bathroom and make myself throw up. Or, um, maybe take some laxatives. \n\nI just tell myself it's only to undo the damage, you know? Just to sort of reset things so I don't gain weight. But... I always feel really embarrassed afterward. And a little shaky, honestly. It's not something I'm proud of at all. \n\nI really try not to do it, though. I know it's probably not the best way to handle things, right? I hope you don't think too badly of me for it... I'm really trying to be honest with you so I can get better. I guess, for the last few months, it's been happening most weeks. Just, like, maybe once a week I'll have one of those nights where the eating gets a little out of control. \n\nAnd then... yeah, I'll try to make up for it. Sometimes I'll make myself throw up, or take those laxatives like I mentioned. But other times I just try to be really good the next day, you know? Like, I'll skip some meals, or I'll force myself to go on a really long walk to try and burn it off. \n\nI just... I want to fix it so I don't ruin all my hard work. That's not too terrible, is it? I mean, at least I'm trying to balance it out, right? I hope that doesn't sound too awful... I really am trying my best here.\n\nDoctor: Do you always make yourself throw up or take laxatives after one of these episodes of eating?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not always. I mean, like I said, I don't always throw up or take the laxatives. Sometimes I just... I try to handle it in a healthier way? I guess. \n\nLike, if I have a night where I eat too much, the next day I'll just be really strict with myself. I'll usually just skip breakfast and lunch entirely. Or... or I'll make sure I go for a really, really long walk before my shift at the store. And I mean, I'm not doing it because I just love walking or anything... it's just, I feel like I absolutely have to, you know? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I'm sorry, I know we keep circling back to this, and I really hope I'm not being difficult or confusing you. But, um, yes, more often than not... actually, pretty much every time. 

Like I've been saying, for the last few months, most weeks I'll have one of those nights where the eating just gets away from me. And when that happens, I always try to make up for it somehow. I just... I can't just leave it. So I'll either throw up, use the laxatives, skip meals the next day, or make myself go on a really long walk to burn it off. 

I always do *something* to balance it out, so I guess the answer is yes, I do it more often than not. Does that answer your question? I really want to make sure I'm giving you the information you need.
